### Q1. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A bead of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> can slide without friction on a fixed circular horizontal ring of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math> having a centre at the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>. The bead is attached to one of the ends of spring of spring constant <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></math>. Natural length of spring is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math> and the other end of the spring is fixed at point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math> as shown in the figure. If the bead is released from position <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>, then the kinetic energy of the bead when it reaches point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> is
<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/335315q.png" style="width: 166px; height: 147px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>25</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msup><mi>kR</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>9</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msup><mi>kR</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><msup><mi>kR</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>12</mn><msup><mi>kR</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><msup><mi>kR</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/tydcoeamfkwnynkzdxoxskooywzupzghogczxnrmggfvywdiyu.jpeg" />

Given,

Mass of bead <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>

Radius of circular horizontal ring <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math>

Natural length of spring <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math>

According to conservation of energy,

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>KE</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>PE</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>KE</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>PE</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mo>  </mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mn>0</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><msup><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi>OA</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>KE</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><msup><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi>OB</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mo>  </mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mn>0</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><msup><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>KE</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><msup><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mo>  </mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msub><mi>KE</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><msup><mi>kR</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>

</details>

### Q2. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A running man has the same kinetic energy as that of a boy of half his mass. The man speed up by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> and the boy changes his speed by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>, so that the kinetic energies of the boy and the man are again equal. Then <math><mi>x</mi></math> in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** 2

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let <math><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow></msub></math> be velocity of man,<math><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow></msub></math>of boy, then kinetic energy according to question ,

<math><mi>i</mi><mi>e</mi><mi> </mi><mi>K</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>M</mi><msubsup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>.</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>.</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup></math>

Or <math><msubsup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

Or <math><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><msqrt><mi> </mi><mn>2</mn></msqrt><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow></msub></math>

When man speeds up <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>and boy changes his speed by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>.Then ,

<math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>M</mi><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>⋅</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>⋅</mo><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

Or <math><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mn>2</mn><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>∵</mo><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>

Or <math><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>x</mi></math>

Or +<math><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi></math>

</details>

### Q3. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A rod <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AB</mi></math> of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> and length <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math> is lying on a horizontal frictionless surface. A particle of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> travelling along the surface hits the end <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> of the rod with a velocity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> in a direction perpendicular to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AB</mi></math>. The collision is perfectly elastic and after the collision, the particle comes to rest. The ratio <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mfrac></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/Q11111111.jpg" style="width: 250px; height: 234px;" />
Using the principle of conservation of linear momentum</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>mv</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>Mv</mi><mi>cm</mi></msub></math></p><p>Using the principle of conservation of angular momentum</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>mv</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>ML</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>12</mn></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">&#969;</mi></math></p><p>Since the collision is completely elastic, the velocity of approach is equal to the velocity of separation</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mi>cm</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#969;</mi><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

### Q4. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A particle is attached to a string and the other end of the string is attached to a fixed support. In order to just complete the vertical circular motion, what should be the ratio of the kinetic energy of the particle at its highest point to that at its lowest point?

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **0.2**

**Detailed Solution:**

K.E <math><mi> </mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi> </mi><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

At lowest point in vertical circular motion <math><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>5</mn><mi>r</mi><mi>g</mi></msqrt></math> and at highest point <math><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>h</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mi>r</mi><mi>g</mi></msqrt></math>

<math><mo>∴</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>K</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>E</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi>h</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>K</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>E</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0.2</mn></math>

</details>

### Q5. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A uniform chain of length <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi></math> and mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>M</mi></math> is lying on a smooth table and one-third of its length is hanging vertically down over the edge of the table. If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi></math> is the acceleration due to gravity, the work required to pull the hanging part on to the table is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>M</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>L</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>M</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>L</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>M</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>L</mi></mrow><mn>9</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>M</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>L</mi></mrow><mn>18</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>M</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>L</mi></mrow><mn>18</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Mass of hanging portion is <math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math> <math><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfrac></math> below the table top.

Therefore, the required work done is,

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image7-a68fc384-bc8a-4ef3-a5b3-0711c1829843.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="height: 143px; width: 290px;" />

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>18</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

---

### Q6. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The bob of a pendulum is released from a horizontal position <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> as shown in the figure. If the length of the pendulum is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>, what is the speed with which the bob arrives at the lowermost point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>, given that it dissipated <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>%</mo></math> of its initial energy against air resistance?

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/mmyeuubnnwyrdvcrrgikcqevpbtsnwyeddvxxrgrrlyfepcajz.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 47px; top: -8px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5.5</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5.3</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4.4</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5.3</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

At the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> the energy of the pendulum is entirely potential energy. At point B, the energy of the pendulum is entirely kinetic energy. It means that as the bob pendulum lowers from A to B, the potential energy is converted into kinetic energy. Thus, at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>, KE = PE. But <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>%</mo></math> of the potential energy is dissipated against air resistance.

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/kxzwevvdqsqaytpybrwjisxdcbjhkjusvuevkjcfxsochsualg.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" />

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KE</mi></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>95</mn><mo>%&#160;</mo><mi>of</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>PE</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>at</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> .... (i)

<math><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi></math> From equation (i), <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>95</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>100</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></math>

<math><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>95</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>100</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>95</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>100</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>9.8</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1.5</mn></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>19</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>9.8</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1.5</mn></mrow><mn>10</mn></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>27.93</mn></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>5.285</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>.3</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

</details>

---

### Q7. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A particle of mass <math><mi mathvariant="bold">m</mi></math> is moving in a circular path of constant radius <math><mi mathvariant="bold">r</mi></math> such that its centripetal acceleration <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="bold">a</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="bold">c</mi></mrow></msub></math> is varying with time <math><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></math> as <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="bold">a</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="bold">c</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> , where <math><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></math> is a constant. The power delivered to the particle by the force acting on it is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** Zero

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

or <math><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

or <math><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></math>

Therefore, tangential acceleration, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></math>

or Tangential force,

<math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></math>

Only tangential force does work.

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">w</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow></mfenced><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

or <math><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">w</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></math>

</details>

### Q8. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A block of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math> is connected rigidly with a smooth wedge (plank) by a light spring of stiffness <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi></math>. If the wedge is moved with constant velocity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>, then find the work done by the external agent until the maximum compression of the spring.

<img alt="" height="128" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0014759_Question_1.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" width="196" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <mi>m</mi><mo>⁡</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>v</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>0</mn> <mn>2</mn> </msubsup> </mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math><mrow><mn>2</mn>  <mi>m</mi><mo>&ApplyFunction;</mo>  <msubsup>        <mrow>          <mi>v</mi><mo>&ApplyFunction;</mo>        </mrow>        <mn>0</mn>        <mn>2</mn>  </msubsup></mrow></math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow><mn>3</mn> <mi>m</mi><mo>⁡</mo> <msubsup> <mrow> <mi>v</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>0</mn> <mn>2</mn> </msubsup> </mrow> </math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow><mn>2</mn> <mi>m</mi><mo>⁡</mo> <msubsup> <mrow> <mi>v</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>0</mn> <mn>3</mn> </msubsup> </mrow> </math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math> <mrow> <mi>m</mi><mo>⁡</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>v</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>0</mn> <mn>2</mn> </msubsup> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let us take wedge + spring + block as a system. The forces responsible for performing work are spring force kx (←) and the external force F(→).

<img alt="" height="110" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0014759_AnsExp_1.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" width="242" />

Work Energy theorem for block + spring + plank relative to ground :

Applying work-energy theorem, we have W<sub>ext</sub> + W<sub>sp</sub> = ΔK

where W<sub>sp</sub> the total work done by the spring on wedge and block <math> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>k</mtext> <msup> <mi>x</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </math> and ΔK = change in KE of the block (because the plank does not change its kinetic energy)

<math> <mrow> <mtext>Then,</mtext> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <msub> <mtext>W</mtext> <mtext>ext</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>k</mtext> <msup> <mi>x</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <mtext>Δ</mtext> <mtext>K</mtext> </mrow> </math>

As the block was initially stationary and it will acquire a velocity v<sub>0</sub> equal to that of the plank at the time of maximum compression of the spring, the change in kinetic energy of the block relative to ground is

<math> <mrow> <mtext>         Δ</mtext> <mtext>K</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mi>m</mi><mo>⁡</mo> <msubsup> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>v</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>0</mn> <mn>2</mn> </msubsup> </mrow> </math>

Substituting ΔK in the above equation, we have

<math> <mrow> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <msub> <mtext>W</mtext> <mtext>ext</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>K</mtext> <msup> <mi>x</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mi>m</mi><mo>⁡</mo> <msubsup> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>v</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>0</mn> <mn>2</mn> </msubsup> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>...(i)</mtext> </mrow> </math>

Work Energy theorem for block + spring + plank  relative to the plank W<sub>ext</sub> + W<sub>sp</sub> = ΔK The plank moves with constant velocity, there is no pseudo-force acting on the block. W<sub>ext</sub> = 0 Then the net work done on the system (block + plank), due to the spring, can be given as

<math> <mrow> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <msub> <mtext>W</mtext> <mtext>SP</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>k</mtext> <msup> <mi>x</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </math>

As the relative velocity between the observer (plank) and block decreases from v<sub>0</sub> to zero at the time of maximum compression of the spring, the change in kinetic energy of the block is <math> <mrow> <mtext>Δ</mtext> <mtext>K</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mi>m</mi><mo>⁡</mo> <msubsup> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>v</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>0</mn> <mn>2</mn> </msubsup> <mtext>.</mtext> </mrow> </math>

Substituting W<span style="font-size: 10.8333px;"><sub>sp  </sub> <span style="font-size:12px;">and</span>  </span><math><mover accent="false"><mrow><mo>△</mo><mi>K</mi></mrow></mover></math> in above equation 
 -<math><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>k</mi><mo>⁡</mo> <msup> <mi>x</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>-</mtext><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mi>m</mi><mo>⁡</mo> <msubsup> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>v</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>0</mn> <mn>2</mn> </msubsup></mrow></math>                                     (ii)

From (i) &amp; (ii)
<math><mrow><msub><mtext>W</mtext> <mtext>ext</mtext> </msub> </mrow></math><math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><msubsup><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><msubsup><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup></math>

</details>

---

### Q9. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

As shown in the figure, a chain of mass <math><mi>m</mi></math> is placed on a smooth quarter circular portion of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math>. End <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A </mi></math>is tied with a wedge while the remaining chain is free, then the minimum work required to be done by the external agent to make the chain horizontal keeping point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> fixed, is
  <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/32472q.png" style="width: 192px; height: 178px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mtext>gR</mtext></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mtext>g</mtext><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mtext>R</mtext></mrow><mi>π</mi></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mtext>g</mtext><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mtext>R</mtext></mrow><mi>&#x3C0;</mi></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** None

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mtext>g</mtext><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mtext>R</mtext></mrow><mi>π</mi></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Center of mass of the chain is at <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow> <mi>π</mi> </mfrac> </mrow> </math> from horizontal

As shown in figure .......

Height gain by center of mass is <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>R</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>π</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>


         <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0032472_Ans.jpg" style="width: 223px; height: 99px;" />


<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>Wg</mtext><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>m</mi><mtext>g</mtext><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mtext>R</mtext></mrow><mi>π</mi></mfrac></mfenced></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mtext>W</mtext><mtext>ext.</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mtext>Wg</mtext><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>m</mi><mtext>g</mtext><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mtext>R</mtext></mrow><mi>π</mi></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>

  <strong>Alternate solution</strong>


          <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/32472q.png" style="width: 192px; height: 178px;" />


<math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>U</mtext> <mtext>i</mtext> </msub><mo>=</mo> <mo>∫</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>dm</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <mtext>gy</mtext> </mrow> </math>

   <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mtext>g</mtext><mo>∫</mo><mtext>R cos</mtext><mi>θ</mi><mfenced><mfrac><mi>m</mi><mfrac><mi>π</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfrac></mfenced><mtext>d</mtext><mi>θ</mi></math>

    <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mtext>Rg</mtext><mi>m</mi></mrow><mi>π</mi></mfrac><munderover><mo>∫</mo><mn>0</mn><mfrac><mi>π</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></munderover><mo>cos</mo><mi>θ</mi><mtext>d</mtext><mi>θ</mi></math>

    <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mtext>Rg</mtext><mi>m</mi></mrow><mi>π</mi></mfrac><mo>[</mo><mtext>sin</mtext><mi>θ</mi><msubsup><mo>]</mo><mn>0</mn><mrow><mi>π</mi><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup></math>

    <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><mtext>gR</mtext></mrow><mi>π</mi></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mtext>U</mtext><mtext>f</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>⇒</mo><mo>Δ</mo><mtext>U</mtext><mo>=</mo><msub><mtext>w</mtext><mtext>ext</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><mtext>gR</mtext></mrow><mi>π</mi></mfrac></math>

</details>

### Q10. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> represent the work done in moving a particle from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> along three different paths <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn></math> respectively (as shown) in the gravitational field of a point mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math>. Find the correct relation between <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>.

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0007534_Ques1_1.jpg" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Gravitational field is a conservative force field. In a conservative force field work done is path independent

</details>

---

### Q11. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

An <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> metal block of dimensions <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>16</mn><mo> </mo><mi>cm</mi><mo>×</mo><mn>8</mn><mo> </mo><mi>cm</mi><mo>×</mo><mn>6</mn><mo> </mo><mi>cm</mi></math> is lying on a table with its face of largest area touching the table. If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi>ms</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>, then the minimum amount of work done in making it stand with its length vertical is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>12</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

When face of the largest area is touching the table, height of centre of gravity above the table <math> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>h</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>6</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mn>3</mn> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>cm</mtext> </mrow> </math>

With its length vertical, height of centre of gravity would become <math> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>h</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>6</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mn>8</mn> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>cm</mtext> </mrow> </math>

Minimum work required W = (P.E.)<sub>2</sub> – (P.E.)<sub>1</sub>

                                         = mg(h<sub>2</sub> – h<sub>1</sub>)

<math> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>W</mi><mo>⁡</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>8</mn> <mo>×</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>8</mn> <mo>-</mo> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mn>4</mn> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>J</mtext> </mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q12. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A rubber ball is dropped from a height of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> on a plane. It bounces back to a height of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>. Find the coefficient of the restitution for the collision.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **0.6**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>If the ball is dropped from height <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi></math> and bounces back to height <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>h</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>h</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>e</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>h</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mi>e</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><msub><mi>h</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mi>h</mi></mfrac></msqrt></math></p><p>       <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow><mn>5</mn></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q13. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A block of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> is released from the top of a rough incline having <math><mrow><mi>μ</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <msqrt> <mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mtext>.</mtext> </mrow></math> The initial speed of the block is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. The inclined plane has unknown length and has a spring of spring constant <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> connected at the base as in the figure. Find the maximum compression of spring. (answer in meter).

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/260421_ques.JPG" style="width: 180px; height: 123px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math> <mrow> <mtext>ma</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>mg sin </mtext> <mn>3</mn> <msup> <mn>0</mn> <mtext>o</mtext> </msup> <mo>-</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <msqrt> <mn>3</mn> </msqrt> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>mg cos </mtext> <mn>6</mn> <msup> <mn>0</mn> <mtext>o</mtext> </msup> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>⇒ </mo> <mtext>a</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </math>

by energy conservation

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/WhatsApp_Image_2020-07-08_at_2.54.58_PM.jpeg" style="width: 200px; height: 132px;" />

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>w</mi><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow></msub><mo> </mo><mo>+</mo><mo> </mo><msub><mi>w</mi><mrow><mi>f</mi><mi>r</mi></mrow></msub><mo> </mo><mo>+</mo><mo> </mo><msub><mi>w</mi><mrow><mi>s</mi><mi>p</mi><mi>r</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow></msub><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo></math><math> <mrow> <mo>Δ</mo> <mtext>K.E.</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="}" open="{"><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>–</mo><msub><mi>w</mi><mrow><mi>f</mi><mi>r</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msup><mtext>kx</mtext><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msup><mtext>mv</mtext><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>⇒</mo><mtext>x</mtext><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mtext>m</mtext><mtext>k</mtext></mfrac></msqrt><mtext>v</mtext><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mtext>m</mtext></math>

</details>

### Q14. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>A chain is held on a frictionless table with  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac bevelled="true"><mn>1</mn><mi>n</mi></mfrac></math>th  of its length hanging over the edge. If the chain has a length <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi></math> and a mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>M</mi></math>, how much work is required to pull the hanging part back on the table?</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>M</mi><mi>g</mi><msup><mi>L</mi><mrow></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>M</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>L</mi></mrow><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>M</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>M</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><msup><mi>n</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>M</mi><mi>g</mi><msup><mi>L</mi><mrow></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<u><strong><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/415416516581.jpg" style="width: 290px; height: 175px;" /></strong></u>

If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math> is the mass per unit length of the chain, <span style="line-height: 1.6em;">the mass of the chain of length <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi></math> will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>×</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>y</mi><mi>m</mi></math> and the force acting on it due to gravity will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>×</mo><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>y</mi></math>(assuming that <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi></math> is the length of the chain hanging over the edge). So, the work done in pulling the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>d</mo><mi>y</mi></math> length of the chain on the table

           <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>d</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>F</mi><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mo>d</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>         [as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi></math> is decreasing]</span>

<span style="line-height: 1.6em;">i.e.,     </span><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>d</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>d</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>)</mo></math><span style="line-height: 1.6em;">   [as</span><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>y</mi></math><span style="line-height: 1.6em;">]</span>

<span style="line-height: 1.6em;">So, the work done in pulling the hanging portion on the table :</span>

 <math> <mrow> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>W</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <msubsup> <mo>∫</mo> <mtext>L/n</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msubsup> <mtext>mgydy</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>MgL</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>2n</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mtext>or</mtext> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>W</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <msup> <mtext>MgL/2n</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mfenced close="]" open="[" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>as  M</mtext> <mo> = </mo> <mtext>mL</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>

</details>

---

### Q15. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The block of mass 'm' initially at x = 0 is acted upon by a horizontal force F = a - bx as shown in the figure. The coefficient of friction between the surfaces of contact is <math> <mrow> <mi>μ</mi> </mrow> </math>. The net work done on the block is zero if the block travels a distance of ________.

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0032564_Question.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 250px; height: 121px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>a</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <mi>&mu;</mi> <mtext>mg</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>/</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>b</mtext> </mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>a</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <mi>&mu;</mi> <mtext>mg</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>/</mo> <mtext>b</mtext> </mrow> </math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>a</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <mi>&mu;</mi> <mtext>mg</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>/</mo> <mtext>b</mtext> </mrow> </math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>a</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mi>&mu;</mi> <mtext>mg</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>/</mo> <mtext>b</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>a</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <mi>&mu;</mi> <mtext>mg</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>/</mo> <mtext>b</mtext> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

f = a - bx

<math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>f</mtext> <msub> <mtext>n</mtext> <mtext>ef</mtext> </msub> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>a</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>bx</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <msub> <mtext>f</mtext> <mtext>s</mtext> </msub> </mrow> </math>

For block to move

at x = 0

<math> <mrow> <mtext>a</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <msub> <mtext>b</mtext> <mtext>x</mtext> </msub> <mo>&gt;</mo> <msub> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>f</mtext> <mtext>s</mtext> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mtext>max</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mi>&mu;</mi> <mtext>mg</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mtext>a</mtext> <mo>&gt;</mo> <mi>&mu;</mi> <mtext>mg</mtext> </mrow> </math>, So we are assuming motion starts at x = 0

<math> <mrow> <mtext>w</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mo>&int;</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>a</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>bx</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <mi>&mu;</mi> <mtext>mg</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <mtext>dx</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mtext>o</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>ax</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>bx</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>-</mo> <mi>&mu;</mi> <mtext>mgx</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&rArr;</mo> <mtext>x</mtext> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>a</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>bx</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>-</mo> <mi>&mu;</mi> <mtext>mg</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </math>

x = 0

<math> <mrow> <mtext>a</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>bx</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>-</mo> <mi>&mu;</mi> <mtext>mg</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>bx</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>a</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <mi>&mu;</mi> <mtext>mg</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mtext>x</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>b</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>a</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <mi>&mu;</mi> <mtext>mg</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>

</details>

---

### Q16. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo> </mo><mo>kg</mo></math> object has initial velocity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>6</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>.</mo></math> What will be the total work done (in joule) on the object if its velocity changes to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>8</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>?

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **60**

**Detailed Solution:**

The net work done on the object is equal to the change in the kinetic energy of the object

<math><msub><mrow><mi>W</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi> </mi><msub><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>Δ</mi><mi>K</mi></math>

Kinetic energy <math><mi>K</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

Velocity <math><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>+</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup></math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mn>36</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><msup> <mtext>m</mtext> <mtext>2</mtext> </msup> <msup> <mrow> <mtext>/s</mtext></mrow> <mtext>2</mtext> </msup> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <msup> <mi>v</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>=</mo><mn>40</mn><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><msup> <mtext>m</mtext> <mtext>2</mtext> </msup> <msup> <mrow> <mtext>/s</mtext></mrow> <mtext>2</mtext> </msup> </mrow> </math>

<math><msub><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>60</mn><mi> </mi><mi>J</mi></math>

Again, velocity <math><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msup><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></msqrt></math>

<math><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>64</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>16</mn></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>80</mn></msqrt><mrow> <mtext> m/s</mtext></mrow></math>

Kinetic energy <math><msub><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>×</mo><mn>80</mn><mrow><mrow> <msup> <mtext> m</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <msup> <mrow> <mtext>/s</mtext></mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mn>120</mn><mi> </mi><mn>J</mn></math>

From work energy theorem

<math><msub><mrow><mi>W</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi> </mi><msub><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow></msub></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mn>120</mn><mi> </mi><mn>J</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>60</mn><mi> </mi><mn>J</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>60</mn><mi> </mi><mn>J</mn></math>

</details>

---

### Q17. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A person of mass <math><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> is sitting on a swing of length <math><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math> and swinging with and angular amplitude <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>.</mo></math> If the person stands up when the swing passes through its lowest point, the work done by him, assuming that his centre of mass moves by a distance <math><mi>l</mi><mi> </mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>l</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo></math> is close to:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi>l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi>l</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi>l</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi>l</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>)</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi>l</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>)</mo></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

From angular momentum conservation 
<math><mtable columnalign="left"><mtr><mtd><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>l</mi><mo>)</mo></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>L</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mtd></mtr></mtable></math>  
Now 
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi>l</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>  
Since <math><mi>l</mi><mo>≪</mo><mi>L</mi></math>  
<math><mtable columnalign="left"><mtr><mtd><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi>l</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi>l</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mfrac><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi>l</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></mtd></mtr></mtable></math>  
From SHM 
<math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">ω</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mo>×</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></msqrt><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math>  
Using this 
<math><mtable columnalign="left"><mtr><mtd><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi>l</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>×</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi>l</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mtd></mtr></mtable></math>

</details>

---

### Q18. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The blocks <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo> </mo></math>and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> shown in the figure have masses <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math>. the system is released from rest. The speed of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> after <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> has travelled a distance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> along the incline is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>  
<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0026944_Question.jpg" style="width: 200px; height: 250px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mfrac><mn>15</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mfrac><mn>15</mn><mn>8</mn></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>6</mn></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>6</mn></mfrac></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> moves down the incline by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> shall move up by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>. If the speed of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi></math> then the speed of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>v</mi></math>.

From the conservation of energy: Gain in K.E. = loss in P.E.

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>M</mi><mtext>A</mtext></msub><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>v</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>M</mi><mtext>B</mtext></msub><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>M</mi><mtext>A</mtext></msub><mtext>g</mtext><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>M</mi><mtext>B</mtext></msub><mtext>g</mtext><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>

Solving we get,   <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msqrt><mfrac><mtext>g</mtext><mn>3</mn></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>6</mn></mfrac></msqrt></math>

</details>

---

### Q19. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A particle is released from a height <math><mi>S</mi></math>. At certain height its kinetic energy is three times its potential energy. The height and speed of the particle at that instant are respectively

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mi>S</mi> <mn>4</mn> </mfrac> <mo>,</mo><mtext> </mtext><mfrac> <mrow> <mn>3</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>S</mi></mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mi>S</mi> <mn>4</mn> </mfrac> <mo>,</mo><mtext> </mtext><mfrac> <mrow> <msqrt> <mrow> <mn>3</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>S</mi></mrow> </msqrt> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>S</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mrow><msqrt><mn>3</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>S</mi></msqrt></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>S</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>S</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>S</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>S</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

We can realize the situation as shown.  Let at point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"> <mi>C</mi> </math> distance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"> <mi>x</mi> </math> from highest point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"> <mi>A</mi> </math>, the particle's kinetic energy is three times its potential energy.

Velocity <math><mi>C</mi><mo>,</mo></math>

<math><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>x</mi></math>

<math><mi>O</mi><mi>r</mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>x</mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi></math>.....(i)

Potential energy at ,<math><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi> </mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>S</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mo>…</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>i</mi><mo>)</mo></math>


<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image21-77280a62-8d67-4094-b093-0338e686eade.jpg" style="width: 137px; height: 224px;" />

At Point C,

Kinetic energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"> <mrow> <mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>×</mo></mrow> </math> potential energy

ie, <math><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi> </mi><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><mo>×</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>×</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>S</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>)</mo></math>

or <math><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>S</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi></math>

or <math><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>S</mi></math>

or <math><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi>S</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>x</mi></math>

or <math><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>S</mi></math>

Therefore, from Eq.(i)

<math><msup><mrow><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi><mo>×</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>S</mi></math>

Or <math><msup><mrow><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>g</mi><mi>S</mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi>o</mi><mi>r</mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>g</mi><mi>S</mi></msqrt></math>

Height of the particle from the ground

<math><mo>=</mo><mi> </mi><mi>S</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>S</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>S</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>s</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

---

### Q20. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A particle is placed at the origin and a force <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>kx</mi></math> is acting on it (where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></math> is a positive constant). IF <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">U</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>, the graph of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">U</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>)</mo></math> versus <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></math> will be (where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">U</mi></math> is the potential energy function)

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0010228_Ans1_1.jpg" style="width: 201px; height: 192px;" />
- **(B)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0010228_Ans2_1.jpg" style="width: 195px; height: 192px;" />
- **(C)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0010228_Ans3_1.jpg" style="width: 206px; height: 211px;" />
- **(D)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0010228_Ans4_1.jpg" style="width: 194px; height: 192px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0010228_Ans1_1.jpg" style="width: 201px; height: 192px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math> <mrow> <mtext>F</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>dU</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>dx</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>      &rArr;</mo> <mo>    &int;</mo> <mtext>dU</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mo>&int;</mo> <mtext>Fdx</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>U</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mo>-</mo> <msub> <mtext>U</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mfenced close="]" open="[" separators=","> <mrow> <mo>&int;</mo> <mtext>kxdx</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>  &rArr;</mo> <mtext>  U</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <msup> <mtext>kx</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </math>


<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0010228_AnsExp1_1.jpg" style="width: 192px; height: 209px;" />
