### Q34. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A <math><mn>0.5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> block slides from <math><mi>A</mi></math> on horizontal track with an initial speed of <math><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>–</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> towards a weightless horizontal spring of length <math><mn>1 </mn><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> and force constant <math><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">N </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>–</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. The part <math><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi></math> of the track is frictionless and the part <math><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi></math> has the coefficient of static and kinetic friction as <math><mn>0.22</mn></math> and <math><mn>0.20</mn></math> respectively. If the distance <math><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi></math> and <math><mi>B</mi><mi>D</mi></math> are <math><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> m</mi></math> and <math><mn>2.14 m </mn></math>respectively, the total distance through which the block moves before it comes to rest completely is -

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/71827q.png" style="width: 285px; height: 97px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>2.5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>4.42</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>4.24</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>2.44</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mn>4.24</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

At <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>&#8211;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></msub><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>0.5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>&#8211;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>0.2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>0.5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>2.14</mn></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>2.25</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>&#8211;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>2.14</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0.11</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0.11</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>k</mi><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></msub><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>0.11</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>2.14</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>0.1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4.24</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>

</details>

---

### Q35. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A wire, suspended vertically from one of its ends, is stretched by attaching a weight of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>200</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math> to the lower end. The weight stretches the wire by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>. The elastic energy (in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>) stored in the wire is

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **0.1**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mi>F</mi><mo>×</mo><mi>l</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mn>200</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mtext>0.1</mtext><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

</details>

---

### Q36. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A man is holding a chain of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math> and length <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>l</mi></math> such that the lower end of the chain just touches a table. Now the man places half the length of the chain very slowly on the table while keeping the other half vertical. The work done by the man in this process is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8211;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8211;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>3</mn><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow><mn>8</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8211;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow><mn>8</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>3</mn><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow><mn>8</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

The work done by man is negative of magnitude of decrease in potential energy of chain.




<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image1-5230aec2-2e12-41bd-8861-89bc53f01816.jpg" />



<math><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">U</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mo>–</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mo>∴</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>–</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="script">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

---

### Q37. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The velocity-time graph of a particle of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> is shown in the figure. The net work done on the particle in the first two seconds of the motion is

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/WhatsApp_Image_2020-05-24_at_8.22.59_PM.jpeg" style="width: 250px; height: 179px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mo>-</mo><mn>9300</mn><mi> </mi><mtext>J</mtext></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>12000</mn><mi> </mi><mtext>J</mtext></math>
- **(C)** <math><mo>-</mo><mn>4500</mn><mi> </mi><mtext>J</mtext></math>
- **(D)** <math><mo>-</mo><mn>12000</mn><mi> </mi><mtext>J</mtext></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mo>-</mo><mn>4500</mn><mi> </mi><mtext>J</mtext></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>50</mn></math>

<math><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>a</mi><mi>t</mi><mi> </mi><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>40</mn></math>

<math><mo>∆</mo><mi> </mi><mi>K</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>E</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>=</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>D</mi><mo>.</mo><mi> </mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><mn>40</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mn>50</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>×</mo><mn>10</mn></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4500</mn></math> <mtext>J</mtext>

</details>

---

### Q38. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A particle is released from a height <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi></math>, At a certain height; its kinetic enegy is two times its potential energy. Height and speed of the particle at that instant are

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></msqrt></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Total mechanical energy= <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></math>

As, <math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi></math>

and <math><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></math>

Height from the ground at this instant,

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>h</mi><mo>,</mo></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>h</mi><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mi>&#160;</mi></math></p><p>and speed of the particle at this instant,

<math><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>h</mi><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#8242;</mi></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></msqrt></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>&#160;</mi></mrow></mfenced></msqrt></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q39. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A block of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> moving on a horizontal surface with speed <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>u</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> enters a rough horizontal patch ranging from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>00</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>. If the retarding force <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>f</mi><mi>r</mi></msub></math> on the block in this range is inversely proportional to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> over this range i.e.
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>f</mi><mi>r</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>k</mi><mi>x</mi></mfrac></math> for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.10</mo><mo>&lt;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.00</mo></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>f</mi><mi>r</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mn>0.10</mn><mtext> </mtext></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mn>2.00</mn></math>
If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math> then, the speed of this block ( in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> ) as it crosses the patch is ( use <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="script">l</mi><mi>n</mi><mtext>   </mtext><mn>20</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math> )

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>∫</mo><msub><mi>f</mi><mi>r</mi></msub><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi><msub><mi>log</mi><mi>e</mi></msub><mo>⁡</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>0.1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1.5</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<math> <mo>∴</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>∆</mo><mi>K</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1.5</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q40. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

If potential energy function for the force between two atoms in a diatomic molecule is approximately given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>U</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mi>x</mi></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>8</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>4</mn></msup></mfrac></math>, where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></math> are constants in standard SI units and <math><mi>x</mi></math> is in metres. Find the dissociation energy of the molecule (in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>). [Take <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>8</mn></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>20</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>4</mn></msup></math>]

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **25**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi>U</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>20</mn></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math><mo>∴</mo><mi> </mi><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mo>⇒</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>9</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>20</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math><mo>⇒</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>20</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
<math><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>∞</mi></math> and <math><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>20</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
Also <math><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>20</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math><mo>∴</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><msub><mrow><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>20</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>20</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
<math><mo>⇒</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><msub><mrow><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>20</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>20</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>25</mn><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
<math><mo>∴</mo></math> Dissociation energy <math><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mrow><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>25</mn><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

</details>

---

### Q41. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A ring of mass m free to slide on a fixed smooth horizontal rod is attached to a particle of mass M kg by a inextensible string of length <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>l</mi></math>. Initially, both M and m are at rest and the string is vertical. A horizontal velocity <math><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math> is imparted to the particle. The maximum height up to which block will rise w.r.t its initial position is (M = 2m)

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/xwppzxnhalzykjffhhhltqdjqbmxiwsmjvhowpeobyiyspvzvw.jpeg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mrow><mn>8</mn><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mfrac><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The reduced mass of the system is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>m</mi><mi>reduced</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>K</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mfenced><msup><mfenced><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mfrac><msup><mfenced><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mfenced><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mn>6</mn><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q42. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A particle of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> is dropped from a height <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><mn>40</mn><mo> </mo><mi>cm</mi></math> on a light horizontal platform fixed to one end of an elastic spring, the other being fixed to a base, as shown in the diagram. The particle collides with the platform and sticks to it. As a result, the spring is compressed by an amount <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi>cm</mi></math>. What is the force constant of the spring?
(Take <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>)

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0022109_Question.jpg" style="width: 180px; height: 157px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>600</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 5px; top: -6px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>800</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 5px; top: -6px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1000</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 5px; top: -6px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1200</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 5px; top: -6px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1000</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 5px; top: -6px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Since the platform is depressed by an amount x, the total work done on the spring is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>h</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>)</mo></math>. This work is stored in the spring in the form of potential energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>k</mi><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>.

Equating the two, we have

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>k</mi><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>h</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> or  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mtext>2&#160;</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">mg</mtext><mfenced><mrow><mi>h</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math>

Given, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>. Substituting these values, we get
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1000</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>.

</details>

---

### Q43. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

The potential energy of a particle of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> moving in the <math><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi></math> plane is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>24</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>)</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>, <math><mi>x</mi></math> and <math><mi>y</mi></math> being in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>metre</mi></math>. If the particle starts from rest from origin, then what is the speed (in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>) of particle at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>?

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **10**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>F</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>&#8706;</mo><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>&#8706;</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mover><mi mathvariant="bold">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>&#8706;</mo><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>&#8706;</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfrac><mover><mi mathvariant="bold">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="bold">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>24</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="bold">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>

<math><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><msub><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1.4</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> along positive <math><mi>x</mi></math>-axis

<math><msub><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>24</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mn>4.8</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> along negative <math><mi>y</mi></math>-axis

<math><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow></msub><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1.4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mn>2.8</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

and <math><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4.8</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>9.6</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msubsup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>+</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

</details>

---

### Q44. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A block of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi>kg</mi></math> is connected to a spring of unknown spring constant k. It is compressed to a distance <math><mi>x</mi> </math> from its equilibrium position and released from rest. After approaching half the distance <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math> from the equilibrium position, it hits another block and comes to rest momentarily, while the other block moves with velocity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi> </mi><mn>3</mn><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. The total initial energy of the spring is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.6</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.8</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.5</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.3</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.6</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

By mechanical energy conservation between compression positions <math><mi>x</mi></math> and <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>k</mi><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>k</mi><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>k</mi><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>k</mi><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>k</mi><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>k</mi><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

On collision with a block at rest

<math><mo>&#8757;</mo></math> Velocities are exchanged <math><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> elastic collision between identical masses.

<math><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi>&#160;</mi></msqrt><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>x</mi></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<math><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> The initial energy of the spring is

<math><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>k</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>k</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>36</mn><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>m</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0.1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.6</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

</details>

---

### Q45. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>A body, starting from rest, is moved along a straight line by a machine delivering constant power. The distance moved by the body in time <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi></math> is proportional to</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mrow></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mrow></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mrow></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mrow></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mrow></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Power, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>F</mi><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo></math><mi>c</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi>t</mi>

Or <math><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>a</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi>t</mi><mi>&#160;</mi></math></p><p>Or <math><msup><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>c</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi>t</mi></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mi>t</mi></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math> ....... (1)

Now, <math><mi>S</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>a</mi><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>&#8757;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>u</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>S</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><mi>a</mi><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>S</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mi>t</mi></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>  [Using (1)]

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>S</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mrow></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q46. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>A vertical spring of force constant <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi>Nm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> is attached to the ceiling, with a block of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> suspended from it. Now an external force <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi></math> is applied on the block so that the spring is stretched by an additional length of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>. The work done by the force <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi></math> is [Take <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi>ms</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>]</p><p>
<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0027622_Question.jpg" style="width: 138px; height: 136px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>200</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>400</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>600</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>200</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

x = elongation in spring due to mass 10 kg

<math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mo>×</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>1 m</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>W</mtext> <mtext>F</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>×</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mo>×</mo> <mfenced close="]" open="[" separators=","> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>-</mo> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mo>×</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mo>×</mo> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </math>

= <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>200</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

</details>

### Q47. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The potential energy for a force field <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mtext mathvariant="italic">F</mtext><mo>→</mo></mover></math> is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>U</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>,</mo><mo> </mo><mi>y</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mi>cos</mi><mo> </mo><mo>(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo> </mo><mo>+</mo><mo> </mo><mi>y</mi><mo>)</mo></math>. The force acting on a particle at the position given by coordinates <math> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>0</mn> <mtext>, </mtext> <mi>π</mi> <mo>/</mo> <mn>4</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> </msqrt> </mrow> </mfrac> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mtext>i</mtext> <mo>^</mo> </mover> <mo>+</mo> <mover accent="true"> <mtext>j</mtext> <mo>^</mo> </mover> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> </msqrt> </mrow> </mfrac> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mtext>i</mtext> <mo>^</mo> </mover> <mo>+</mo> <mover accent="true"> <mtext>j</mtext> <mo>^</mo> </mover> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mover accent="true"> <mtext>i</mtext> <mo>^</mo> </mover> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msqrt> <mn>3</mn> </msqrt> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mover accent="true"> <mtext>j</mtext> <mo>^</mo> </mover> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mover accent="true"> <mtext>i</mtext> <mo>^</mo> </mover> <mo>-</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msqrt> <mn>3</mn> </msqrt> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mover accent="true"> <mtext>j</mtext> <mo>^</mo> </mover> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> </msqrt> </mrow> </mfrac> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mtext>i</mtext> <mo>^</mo> </mover> <mo>+</mo> <mover accent="true"> <mtext>j</mtext> <mo>^</mo> </mover> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>F</mtext> <mtext>x</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mo>&part;</mo> <mtext>U</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mo>&part;</mo> <mtext>x</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>sin</mtext> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>x</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mtext>y</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>,  <math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>F</mtext> <mtext>y</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mo>&part;</mo> <mtext>U</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mo>&part;</mo> <mtext>x</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>sin</mtext> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>x</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mtext>y</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>F</mtext> <mtext>x</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>sin</mtext> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>x</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mtext>y</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <msub> <mo>]</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>0</mn> <mtext>, </mtext> <mi>&pi;</mi> <mo>/</mo> <mn>4</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> </msqrt> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>,

<math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>F</mtext> <mtext>y</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>sin</mtext> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>x</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mtext>y</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <msub> <mo>]</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>0</mn> <mtext>, </mtext> <mi>&pi;</mi> <mo>/</mo> <mn>4</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> </msqrt> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&there4;   </mo> <mtext>F</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> </msqrt> </mrow> </mfrac> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mtext>i</mtext> <mo>^</mo> </mover> <mo>+</mo> <mover accent="true"> <mtext>j</mtext> <mo>^</mo> </mover> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q48. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A spherical ball of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math> is kept at the highest point in the space between two fixed, concentric spheres <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> (see fig.). The smaller sphere <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> has a radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi></math> and space between the two spheres has a width <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>d</mi></math>. The ball has a diameter very slightly less than <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>d</mi></math>. All surfaces are frictionless. The ball is given a gentle push (towards the right in the figure). The angle made by the radius vector of the ball with the upward vertical is denoted by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>θ</mi></math>.  What is the total normal reaction force exerted by the spheres on the ball in terms of angle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>θ</mi></math>?

<img alt="" height="124" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0007554_Ques1_1.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" width="166" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8211;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8211;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8211;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>)</mo></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 105px; top: 37.6667px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>)</mo></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>R</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>d</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mtext>cos</mtext><mi>&#160;&#952;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
 
<img alt="" height="186" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0007554_AnsExp1_1.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" width="172" />
 
The velocity of the ball at an angle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#952;</mi></math> is

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>&#957;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi>R</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>d</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mtext>cos</mtext><mi>&#160;&#952;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>g</mi></math>  ...... (i)

Let N be the normal reaction (away from the centre) at angle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#952;</mi></math>. Then, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext mathvariant="italic">mg&#160;</mtext><mtext>cos</mtext><mi>&#160;&#952;</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>N</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>&#957;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mfenced><mrow><mi>R</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>d</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mfrac></math>

Substituting the value of <math> <mrow> <msup> <mi>&#957;</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </math> from equation (i), we get

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext mathvariant="italic">mg</mtext><mtext>&#160;cos</mtext><mi>&#160;&#952;</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>N</mi><mo>=</mo><mtext>2</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">mg</mtext><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mtext>cos</mtext><mi>&#160;&#952;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>N</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mfenced><mrow><mtext>3 cos</mtext><mi>&#160;&#952;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

</details>

---

### Q49. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>The initial configuration of the system is as shown in the figure.

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/260423_qrghu.png" style="width: 170px; height: 144px;" />

The string and the pulley are light. The bob is released and the string starts wrapping around the pulley. (The pulley is held in place by a force applied at the centre)

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/jee_2phy.JPG" style="width: 150px; height: 152px;" />

Find the rate <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></math> at which the length of the string wrapped around the pulley increases (as a function of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></math>).</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>l</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>R&#x3B8;</mi></mrow></mfenced></mfrac><msqrt><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>l</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>R&#x3B8;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>sin&#x3B8;</mi></mrow></mfenced></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>l</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>R&#x3B8;</mi></mrow></mfenced></mfrac><msqrt><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>l</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>R&#x3B8;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>sin&#x3B8;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>Rcos&#x3B8;</mi></mrow></mfenced></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>l</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>R&#x3B8;</mi></mrow></mfenced></mfrac><msqrt><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>l</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>R&#x3B8;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>sin&#x3B8;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#x3B8;</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>l</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>R&#x3B8;</mi></mrow></mfenced></mfrac><msqrt><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>l</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>R&#x3B8;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>sin&#x3B8;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>cos&#x3B8;</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></msqrt></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>l</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>R&#x3B8;</mi></mrow></mfenced></mfrac><msqrt><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>l</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>R&#x3B8;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>sin&#x3B8;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>cos&#x3B8;</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>At any instant of time, the length of the string wrapped on the pulley is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R&#952;</mi></math> and the remaining length <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>l</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>R&#952;</mi></math></p><p>The rate <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mfrac><mi>d&#952;</mi><mi>dt</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>R&#969;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>l</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>R&#952;</mi></mrow></mfenced></mfrac></math>, where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></math> is the speed of the particle</p><p>The height through which the particle has come down</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>cos&#952;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>l</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>R&#952;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>sin&#952;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>cos&#952;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>R&#952;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>sin&#952;</mi></mrow></mfenced></msqrt></math></p><p>So,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>l</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>R&#952;</mi></mrow></mfenced></mfrac><msqrt><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>l</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>R&#952;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>sin&#952;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>cos&#952;</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></msqrt></math></p>

</details>

### Q50. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>A ball is dropped on to a horizontal plate from a height <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> above it. If the coefficient of restitution is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>e</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac bevelled="true"><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>, the total distance travelled before the ball comes to rest is</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 78px; top: 37.6667px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>15</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 78px; top: 37.6667px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 78px; top: 37.6667px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>25</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 78px; top: 37.6667px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>15</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 78px; top: 37.6667px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>**

**Detailed Solution:**

The impact between the ball and the plate is direct impact. If u is the velocity of the ball before impact, its velocity after is eu, where e is the coefficient of restitution.

But <math> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>u</mi><mo>⁡</mo> <mo>=</mo> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>gh</mtext> </msqrt> </mrow> </math> since the ball falls from a height h. So the velocity of the ball at the first rebound

<math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <msub> <mrow> <mtext>v</mtext> </mrow> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>e</mi><mo>⁡</mo> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>u</mi><mo>⁡</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>e</mi><mo>⁡</mo> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>gh</mtext> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>

The height h<sub>1</sub> to which it rises after first rebound

<math> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>h</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msubsup> <mrow> <mtext>v</mtext> </mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>2</mn> </msubsup> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>g</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>e</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>·</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>gh</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>g</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <msup> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>e</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>h</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> </math>

The velocity v<sub>2</sub> after second rebound v<sub>2</sub> = ev<sub>1</sub> = e<sup>2</sup>u

The height h<sub>2</sub> it rises after second rebound

<math> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>h</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msubsup> <mrow> <mtext>v</mtext> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mn>2</mn> </msubsup> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>g</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>e</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>4</mn> </msup> <mo>·</mo> <msup> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>u</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>g</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>e</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>4</mn> </msup> <mo>·</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>gh</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>g</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <msup> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>e</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>4</mn> </msup> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>h</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> </math>

In general, the height hn to which the ball rises after n<sup>th</sup> rebound is given by h<sub>n</sub> = e<sup>2n</sup>h.

Total distance travelled

    = h + 2h<sub>1</sub> + 2h<sub>2</sub> + 2h<sub>3</sub> + ................+2h<sub>n</sub>+............to ∞

    = h + 2 (e<sup>2</sup>h + e<sup>4</sup>h + e<sup>6</sup> h +.......)

    = h + 2he<sup>2</sup>(1 + e<sup>2</sup> + e<sup>4</sup>+...........)

    <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>h</mi><mo>⁡</mo> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>h</mi><mo>⁡</mo> <msup> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>e</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>-</mo> <msup> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>e</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>h</mi><mo>⁡</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <msup> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>e</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>-</mo> <msup> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>e</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>h</mi><mo>⁡</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>+</mo> <msup> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>e</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>-</mo> <msup> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>e</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

    <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>9</mn> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>+</mo> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>-</mo> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mn>5</mn> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>m</mtext> </mrow> </math>

</details>

---

### Q51. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A ball whose kinetic energy is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi></math>, is thrown at an angle of <math> <mrow> <mn>45</mn><mo>°</mo> </mrow> </math>  with the horizontal. Its kinetic energy at the highest point of its flight will be:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mi>E</mi> <mn>2</mn> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
- **(C)** <math>  <mrow>   <mfrac>    <mi>E</mi>    <mrow>     <msqrt>      <mn>2</mn>     </msqrt>         </mrow>   </mfrac>     </mrow> </math>
- **(D)** 0

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mi>E</mi> <mn>2</mn> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<div><p align="left" style="text-align:left;"><span>Kinetic energy is <i>E</i></span></p><p align="left" style="text-align:left;"><i><span style="position:relative;top:0.0pt"> <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mn>1</mn> <mn>2</mn> </mfrac> <mtext> </mtext><mi>m</mi><msup> <mi>v</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mtext> </mtext><mo>=</mo><mtext> </mtext><mi>E</mi><mtext> </mtext><mo>⇒</mo><mtext> </mtext><mi>v</mi><mtext> </mtext><mo>=</mo><mtext> </mtext><msqrt> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn><mi>E</mi> </mrow> <mi>m</mi> </mfrac> </mrow> </msqrt> </mrow> </math> </span></i></p><p align="left" style="text-align:left;"><span>Velocity at highest point <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo><mi>v</mi><mtext> </mtext><mi>cos</mi><mtext> </mtext><mn>45</mn><mo>°</mo> </mrow> </math> </span></p><p align="left" style="text-align:left;"><span style="position:relative;top:0.0pt"> <math> <mrow> <mo>∴</mo><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mi>v</mi><mtext> </mtext><mi>cos</mi><mtext> </mtext><mn>45</mn><mo>°</mo><mtext> </mtext><mo>=</mo><mtext> </mtext><msqrt> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn><mi>E</mi> </mrow> <mi>m</mi> </mfrac> </mrow> </msqrt> <mtext> </mtext><mo>.</mo><mtext> </mtext><mfrac> <mn>1</mn> <mrow> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> </msqrt> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext> </mtext><mo>=</mo><mtext> </mtext><msqrt> <mrow> <mfrac> <mi>E</mi> <mi>m</mi> </mfrac> </mrow> </msqrt> </mrow> </math> </span><span> 
[ <math> <mo>∵</mo> </math>  at the highest point there is only horizontal velocity]</span></p><p align="left" style="text-align:left;"><span style="position:relative;top:0.0pt"> <math> <mrow> <mo>∴</mo><mtext> </mtext> </mrow> </math> </span><span>K.E. at highest point  <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo><mtext> </mtext><mfrac> <mn>1</mn> <mn>2</mn> </mfrac> <mtext> </mtext><mo>×</mo><mtext> </mtext><mi>m</mi><mtext> </mtext><mfrac> <mi>E</mi> <mi>m</mi> </mfrac> <mtext> </mtext><mo>=</mo><mtext> </mtext><mfrac> <mi>E</mi> <mn>2</mn> </mfrac> </mrow> </math> </span></p></div>

</details>

### Q52. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The figure shows two blocks of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math> each, connected by an ideal unstretched spring and then placed on a frictionless floor. If the blocks are given velocities <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> as shown, then the maximum extension in the spring is

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/tqczhvknnpghljzpdntjrakerwjqzukoanintmgzmfixhtibht.jpeg" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></mrow><mi>k</mi></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>9</mn><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** None of these

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>9</mn><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ixbszwsfuokdlvdqxfsoxnijqynfwtmkarvbksuuzdygryxfhd.jpeg" />

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/syfgsntfvqxbtwjqtwnpzqlbuhmavebcxsjywzbqvpuzqnbali.jpeg" />

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>m</mi><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>

<math><msub><mrow><mi>w</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>s</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>k</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi>k</mi><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>9</mn><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mn>9</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></mrow><mi>k</mi></mfrac></msqrt></math></p>

</details>

### Q53. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A body is projected at an angle of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>60</mn><mo>°</mo></math> with the horizontal. If its kinetic energy at its maximum height is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>, then the height at which its potential energy and kinetic energy have equal values is (consider potential energy at the point of projection to be zero)

**Options:**

- **(A)** half of the maximum height
- **(B)** two third of the maximum height
- **(C)** one sixth of the maximum height
- **(D)** insufficient data to solve the problem

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) two third of the maximum height**

**Detailed Solution:**

KE at maximum height

<math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <msup> <mtext>mu</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <msup> <mtext>cos</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mn>6</mn> <msup> <mn>0</mn> <mo>∘</mo> </msup> <mo>=</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mo>J</mo> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <msup> <mtext>mu</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>=</mo> <mn>4</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mo>J</mo> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>Mg</mo> <msub> <mtext>H</mtext> <mtext>max</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mn>3</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mo>J</mo> </mrow> </math>     ...(i)

When <math> <mrow> <mtext>PE</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>KE</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>⇒   </mo> <mtext>mgh</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mo>=</mo> </mrow> </math>          ...(ii)

from (i) and (ii)

<math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>h</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>H</mtext> <mtext>max</mtext> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

</details>
