### Q54. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A <math><mn>0.5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> block slides from <math><mi>A</mi></math> on horizontal track with an initial speed of <math><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>–</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> towards a weightless horizontal spring of length <math><mn>1 </mn><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> and force constant <math><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">N </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>–</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. The part <math><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi></math> of the track is frictionless and the part <math><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi></math> has the coefficient of static and kinetic friction as <math><mn>0.22</mn></math> and <math><mn>0.20</mn></math> respectively. If the distance <math><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi></math> and <math><mi>B</mi><mi>D</mi></math> are <math><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> m</mi></math> and <math><mn>2.14 m </mn></math>respectively, the total distance through which the block moves before it comes to rest completely is -

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/71827q.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 200px; height: 68px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>2.5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>4.42</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>4.24</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>2.44</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mn>4.24</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

At <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>&#8211;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></msub><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>0.5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>&#8211;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>0.2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>0.5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>2.14</mn></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>2.25</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>&#8211;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>2.14</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0.11</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0.11</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>k</mi><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></msub><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>0.11</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>2.14</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>0.1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4.24</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>

</details>

### Q55. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A block of mass <math><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> with a semicircular track of radius <math><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1.1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> rests on a horizontal frictionless surface. A uniform cylinder of radius <math><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> and mass <math><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1.0</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> is released from rest from the top point <math><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>. The cylinder slips on the semicircular frictionless track. The speed of the block when the cylinder reaches the bottom of the track at <math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image65-ac2448c3-c6df-4af6-bb63-ed0d1610061d.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 140px; height: 100px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mfrac><mn>10</mn><mn>3 </mn></mfrac></msqrt><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3&#160;</mn></mfrac></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2&#160;</mn></mfrac></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>10</mn></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mfrac><mn>10</mn><mn>3 </mn></mfrac></msqrt><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the speed of the block be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi></math>. Then from conservation of momentum, the velocity of the cylinder will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>v</mi></math> in the opposite direction <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>M</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>

Now from conservation of energy

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>M</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>v</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>r</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>

so <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mn>10</mn><mn>3 </mn></mfrac></msqrt><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

</details>

### Q56. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A block of mass <math><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> is kept at the origin at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and is having velocity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><msqrt><mn>5</mn></msqrt><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> in the positive <math><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></math> -direction. The only force on it is a conservative and its potential energy is defined as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>15</mn></math>. Its velocity when the force acting on it is minimum (after the time <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> ) is -

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **8**

**Detailed Solution:**

At <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>80</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>80</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>15</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>K</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>95</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn><mi>x</mi></math>

For <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>

At <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>K</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>U</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>95</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>24</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>15</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>8</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

</details>

---

### Q57. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Two spherical bodies of the same mass <math><mi>M</mi></math>, are moving with velocities <math><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></math>and <math><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> respectively, along the same line. These bodies collide perfectly inelastically and head-on. The loss in kinetic energy is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>M</mi><mo>(</mo><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>M</mi><mo>(</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>-</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>M</mi><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>2</mn><mi>M</mi><mo>(</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>-</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>)</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>M</mi><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Loss of kinetic energy =<math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi> </mi><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>×</mo><mi>M</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>M</mi></mrow></mfrac><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>⋅</mo><mi>M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>M</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math>

=<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>M</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>(</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msup><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup></math>

</details>

### Q58. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>One end of a light spring of natural length <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></math> and spring constant <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></math> is fixed on a rigid wall and the other is fixed to a smooth ring of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> which can slide without friction in a vertical rod fixed at a distance d from the wall. Initially, the spring makes an angle of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>37</mn><mo>°</mo></math> with the horizontal as shown in the diagram. When the system is released from rest, find the speed of the ring when the spring becomes horizontal. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>sin</mi><mo> </mo><mn>37</mn><mo>°</mo><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><mn>3</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>)</mo></math>

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0013960_AnsExp_1_.jpg" style="width: 180px; height: 161px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <mtext>d</mtext> <msqrt> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>3g</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>2d</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>k</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>16m</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math><mrow>  <mtext>d</mtext>  <msqrt>    <mfrac>      <mrow>        <mtext>2g</mtext>      </mrow>      <mrow>        <mtext>3d</mtext>      </mrow>    </mfrac>    <mo>-</mo>    <mfrac>      <mrow>        <mtext>k</mtext>      </mrow>      <mrow>        <mtext>21m</mtext>      </mrow>    </mfrac>  </msqrt></mrow></math>
- **(C)** <math><mrow>  <mtext>d</mtext>  <msqrt><mo>-</mo>    <mfrac>      <mrow>        <mtext>4g</mtext>      </mrow>      <mrow>        <mtext>2d</mtext>      </mrow>    </mfrac>    <mo>+</mo>    <mfrac>      <mrow>        <mtext>k</mtext>      </mrow>      <mrow>        <mtext>12m</mtext>      </mrow>    </mfrac>  </msqrt></mrow></math>
- **(D)** <math><mrow>  <mtext>d</mtext>  <msqrt><mo>-</mo>    <mfrac>      <mrow>        <mtext>8g</mtext>      </mrow>      <mrow>        <mtext>4d</mtext>      </mrow>    </mfrac>    <mo>+</mo>    <mfrac>      <mrow>        <mtext>k</mtext>      </mrow>      <mrow>        <mtext>28m</mtext>      </mrow>    </mfrac>  </msqrt></mrow></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math> <mrow> <mtext>d</mtext> <msqrt> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>3g</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>2d</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>k</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>16m</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

If <math> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>l</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> </math> is the stretched length of the spring

<math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>d</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>l</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <msup> <mtext>cos 37</mtext> <mtext>o</mtext> </msup> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>,</mtext> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>i.e.,</mtext> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>l</mi><mo>⁡</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>d</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mtext>So, the stretch</mtext> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>y</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mi></mi><mi>l</mi><mo>⁡</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>d</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>d</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>d</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>d</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mtext>and</mtext> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>AB</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>h</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>l</mi><mo>⁡</mo> <msup> <mo>sin 37</mo> <mtext>o</mtext> </msup> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>d</mtext> <mo>×</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>d</mtext> </mrow> </math>

Now taking point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> as a reference level and applying the law of conservation of Energy between <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>,

                <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></msub></math>

<math> <mrow> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>mgh</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <msup> <mtext>ky</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo>+</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <msup> <mtext>mv</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mfenced close="]" open="[" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>as for B, h</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>and y</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mtext>or</mtext> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>mgd</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>k</mtext> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>d</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <msup> <mtext>mv</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mfenced close="]" open="[" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>as for A, h</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>d</mtext> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>and y</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>d</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mtext>or</mtext> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>v</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>d</mtext> <msqrt> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>3g</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>2d</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>k</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>16m</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q59. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

The potential energy of a <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> particle, free to move along the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></math>-axis is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>(</mo><mfrac><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mn>4</mn></msup><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>)</mo><mo> </mo><mtext>J</mtext></math>. The total mechanical energy of the particle is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math> then, the maximum speed (in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>) is

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1.5**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Total energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math> It is fixed. For maximum speed, kinetic energy is maximum The potential energy should, therefore, be minimum

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>∵</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mn>4</mn></msup><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>  

or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>⁡</mo></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>(</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math>

For <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> to be minimum,<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>⁡</mo></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>  

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>∴</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>(</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">,</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mi>or</mi><mo> </mo><mo>⁡</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">,</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mo>±</mo><mn>1</mn></math>  </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"></math>at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>At <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>±</mo><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">,</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo> </mo><mtext>J</mtext></math>  

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>∴</mo><mo>(</mo><mtext>Kinetic energy</mtext><msub><mo>)</mo><mtext>max</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><msub><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>⁡</mo></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><msub><mo>⁡</mo><mtext>min</mtext></msub></math>  

or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>∴</mo><mo>(</mo><mtext>Kinetic energy</mtext><msub><mo>)</mo><mtext>max</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>9</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo> </mo><mtext>J</mtext></math> 

or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>⁡</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">ν</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>⁡</mo></mrow><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>9</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math> 

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">ν</mi><mtext>m</mtext><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>9</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mtext>m</mtext><mo>×</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">ν</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>⁡</mo></mrow><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>9</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mo>×</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>9</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>∴</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">ν</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>⁡</mo></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>

### Q60. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A massless platform is kept on a light elastic spring, as shown in the figure. When particle of mass 0.1 kg is dropped on the pan from a height of 0.24 m, the particle sticks to the pan, and the spring is compressed by 0.01 m. From what height should the particle be dropped to cause a compression of 0.04 m?

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0016150_Question_1.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 115px; height: 159px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** 0.96 m
- **(B)** 2.96 m
- **(C)** 3.96 m
- **(D)** 0.48 m

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 3.96 m**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the particle be dropped from a height h and the spring be compressed by y. According to conservation of mechanical energy,<br br="" />Loss in potential energy of the particle = gain in elastic potential energy of the spring

<math> <mrow> <mo>&there4;</mo> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>mg</mtext> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>h</mi><mo>⁡</mo> <mo>+</mo> <mi>y</mi> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mi>k</mi> <msup> <mi>y</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </math>

Now, as the particle and spring remain same, hence

<math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>h</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mo>+</mo> <msub> <mi>y</mi> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>h</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mo>+</mo> <msub> <mi>y</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mi>y</mi> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mi>y</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>0</mn> <mo>&middot;</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mn>4</mn> <mo>+</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo>&middot;</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>h</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mo>+</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo>&middot;</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mn>4</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>=</mo> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>0</mn> <mo>&middot;</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>0</mn> <mo>&middot;</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mn>4</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </math>

Solving we get, h<sub>2</sub> = 3.96 m

</details>

---

### Q61. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A particle of mass <math> <mtext>m</mtext> </math> is driven by a machine that delivers a constant power <math> <mtext>k</mtext> </math> watts. If the particle starts from rest the force on the particle at time <math> <mtext>t</mtext> </math> is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <msqrt> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>m</mi><mi>k</mi></mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mfrac> </mrow> </msqrt> <mtext> </mtext><msup> <mi>t</mi> <mrow> <mo>−</mo><mfrac> <mn>1</mn> <mn>2</mn> </mfrac> </mrow> </msup> </mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math><msqrt><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math> <mrow> <msqrt> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>m</mi><mi>k</mi></mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mfrac> </mrow> </msqrt> <mtext> </mtext><msup> <mi>t</mi> <mrow> <mo>−</mo><mfrac> <mn>1</mn> <mn>2</mn> </mfrac> </mrow> </msup> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></math>

<math><mrow><mo stretchy="false">∫</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">∫</mo><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></math>

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></msqrt></math>

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup></math>

</details>

---

### Q62. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The potential energy of a particle of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> moving in the x - y plane is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>24</mn><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>.</mo></math> <math><mi>x</mi></math> and <math><mi>y</mi></math> are in meter. If the particle starts from rest from origin then speed of the particle at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>14</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>17.5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>&#8706;</mo><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>&#8706;</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi>&#160;</mi><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>&#8706;</mo><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>&#8706;</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi>&#160;</mi><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>24</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover></math>

<math><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>F</mi><mi>x</mi></msub><mi>m</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>7</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1.4</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math><msub><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>24</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.8</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> along negative y-axis

<math><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> <math><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow></msub><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1.4</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.8</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> along negative y-axis

And <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>y</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4.8</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>.6</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><msqrt><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>+</mo><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

</details>

---

### Q63. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If a man of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> jumps to the ground from a height <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi></math> and his centre of mass moves a distance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></math> in the time taken by him to hit the ground, then the average force acting on him is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Here, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi>Mgh</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>Mgh</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mfrac></math>

</details>

---

### Q64. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

Consider an elliptically shaped rail PQ in the vertical plane with <math><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> and <math><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> . A block of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> is pulled along the rail from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi></math> with a force of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>18</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>, which is always parallel to line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>PQ</mi></math> (see the figure given). Assuming no frictional losses, the kinetic energy of the block when it reaches <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi><mo> </mo></math>is <math><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>×</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>)</mo></math> joules. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></math> is (take acceleration due to gravity = <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>–</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> )

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image17-5aae0c64-3590-429e-843c-1ceabe1ff5bf.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="height: 123px; width: 120px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **5**

**Detailed Solution:**

Using work-energy theorem

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>F</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>K</mi><mi>E</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>F</mi><mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>K</mi><mi>E</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>18</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mn>5</mn></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>K</mi><mi>E</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>K</mi><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>50</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q65. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A heavy, flexible uniform chain of length <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mi>r</mi></math> and mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>λ</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mi>r</mi><mo> </mo></math>lies in a smooth semicircular tube <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AB</mi><mo> </mo></math>of the radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi></math>. Assuming a slight disturbance to start the chain in motion, find the velocity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi></math> with which it will emerge from the end <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> of the tube.

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0014750_Question_1_1.jpg" style="width: 249px; height: 164px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>4</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mtext>&#960;</mtext></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mtext>&#960;</mtext><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>3</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>r</mi><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mtext>&#960;</mtext></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mtext>&#960;</mtext><mn>5</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>g</mtext> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>r</mi><mo>⁡</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>π</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>π</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>5</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>r</mi><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mtext>π</mtext></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mtext>π</mtext><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></msqrt></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math> <mrow> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>g</mtext> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>r</mi><mo>⁡</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>π</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>π</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Since friction absent, we can apply the law of conservation of energy. Centre of gravity of a semicircular arc is at a distance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi></mrow><mi>r</mi></mfrac></mfenced></math> from the centre.

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>Initial potential energy</mtext><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mtext mathvariant="italic">λ</mtext><mtext>π</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">r</mtext></mrow></mfenced><mi>g</mi><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mtext mathvariant="italic">r</mtext></mrow><mtext>π</mtext></mfrac></mfenced></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>Final potential energy</mtext><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mtext mathvariant="italic">λ</mtext><mtext>π</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">r</mtext></mrow></mfenced><mi>g</mi><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mtext>π</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">r</mtext></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced></math>

When the chain is completely slipped off the tube, all the links of the chain have the same velocity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi></math>.

<u><strong><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0014750_AnsExp1_1.jpg" /></strong></u>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>Kinetic energy of chain</mtext><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mtext mathvariant="italic">λ</mtext><mtext>π</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">r</mtext></mrow></mfenced><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>From COE,</mtext><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mtext mathvariant="italic">λ</mtext><mtext>π</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">rg</mtext><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mtext>π</mtext></mrow><mtext>r</mtext></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mtext mathvariant="italic">λ</mtext><mtext>π</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">r</mtext></mrow></mfenced><mtext mathvariant="italic">g</mtext><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mtext>π</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">r</mtext></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mtext mathvariant="italic">λ</mtext><mtext>π</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">r</mtext></mrow></mfenced><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>From which we find,</mtext><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>r</mi><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mtext>π</mtext></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mtext>π</mtext><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></msqrt></math>

</details>

---

### Q66. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

Power applied to a particle varies with time as <math><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math> watt, where <math><mi>t</mi></math> is in second. What is the change in its kinetic energy (in joule) between <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>?

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **46**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mo>∴</mo><mi> </mi><mi>d</mi><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo><mi>d</mi><mi>t</mi></math>

<math><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><munderover><mo stretchy="false">∫</mo><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></munderover><mrow><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo><mi>d</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mrow></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></msubsup></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mo>[</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>]</mo></math>

<math><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>56</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>46</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

</details>

### Q67. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A disc of radius <math><mn>0.1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> rolls without sliding on a horizontal surface with a velocity of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. It then ascends a smooth continuous track as shown in the figure. The height (in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>) up to which it will ascend is : ( <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> )

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image2-11c24d31-1ac2-435b-b574-543609e3b34a.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="height: 53px; width: 120px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2.7**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>I</mi><msup><mrow><mi>w</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi><mi> </mi></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>.</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mn>36</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>×</mo><mi>h</mi></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mn>27</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>h</mi><mo>⇒</mo><mn>2.7</mn></math>

</details>

### Q68. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A block of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math> is gently placed over a massive plank moving horizontally over a smooth surface with velocity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10 </mn><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. The coefficient of friction between the block and the plank is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn></math>. The distance travelled by the block till it slides on the plank is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10 </mn><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>15</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>25</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>35</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mn>25</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/D-3_solu.png" style="width: 250px; height: 62px;" />

Initially the block is not moving with respect to ground while plank is moving, it means, relative motion is there between the plank and block due to which friction (kinetic) starts acting. Initial velocity of block is zero and final velocity is <math><mn>10&#160;</mn><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mrow></math> and let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>s</mi></math> is the distance travelled by the block over the ground, then:-

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mn>10</mn></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mn>0</mn></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.2</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>s</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>s</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>s</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>25</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>

</details>

### Q69. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A chain (mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>M</mi></math>) is hanging from a wooden structure as shown in the figure <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>. It is slowly taken to a state as shown in the figure <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>. Calculate the work done by gravity in the process (assume that after reaching the ground, the chain does not form a heap)

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/New_Diagram.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 301px; height: 159px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mrow>  <mfrac>    <mrow>      <mtext>Mg</mtext>    </mrow>    <mrow>      <mn>4</mn>      <mtext>a</mtext>      <mo>+</mo>      <mi>&pi;</mi>      <mtext>b</mtext>    </mrow>  </mfrac>  <mfenced open="[" close="]" separators=",">    <mrow>      <mn>4</mn>      <msup>              <mtext>a</mtext>            <mn>2</mn>      </msup>      <mo>+</mo>      <msup>              <mtext>b</mtext>            <mn>2</mn>      </msup>      <mo>+</mo>      <mtext>a</mtext>      <mtext>b</mtext>      <mi>&pi;</mi>    </mrow>  </mfenced></mrow></math>
- **(B)** <math><mrow>  <mfrac>    <mrow>      <mtext>Mg</mtext>      <mtext>b</mtext>    </mrow>    <mrow>      <mn>4</mn>      <mtext>a</mtext>      <mo>+</mo>      <mi>&pi;</mi>      <mtext>b</mtext>    </mrow>  </mfrac>  <mfenced open="[" close="]" separators=",">    <mrow>      <mtext>b</mtext>      <mo>-</mo>      <mtext>a</mtext>      <mi>&pi;</mi>    </mrow>  </mfenced></mrow></math>
- **(C)** <math><mrow>  <mfrac>    <mrow>      <mn>2</mn>      <mtext>Mg</mtext>    </mrow>    <mrow>      <mn>2</mn>      <mtext>a</mtext>      <mo>+</mo>      <mi>&pi;</mi>      <mtext>b</mtext>    </mrow>  </mfrac>  <mfenced open="[" close="]" separators=",">    <mrow>      <mn>4</mn>      <msup>              <mtext>a</mtext>            <mn>2</mn>      </msup>      <mo>-</mo>      <msup>              <mtext>b</mtext>            <mn>2</mn>      </msup>      <mo>+</mo>      <mtext>ab</mtext>      <mi>&pi;</mi>    </mrow>  </mfenced></mrow></math>
- **(D)** <math><mrow>  <mfrac>    <mrow>      <mtext>Mg</mtext>      <mtext>b</mtext>    </mrow>    <mrow>      <mn>4</mn>      <mtext>a</mtext>      <mo>+</mo>      <mi>&pi;</mi>      <mtext>b</mtext>    </mrow>  </mfrac>  <mfenced open="[" close="]" separators=",">    <mrow>      <mtext>b</mtext>      <mo>+</mo>      <mtext>a</mtext>      <mi>&pi;</mi>    </mrow>  </mfenced></mrow></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mrow>  <mfrac>    <mrow>      <mtext>Mg</mtext>      <mtext>b</mtext>    </mrow>    <mrow>      <mn>4</mn>      <mtext>a</mtext>      <mo>+</mo>      <mi>&pi;</mi>      <mtext>b</mtext>    </mrow>  </mfrac>  <mfenced open="[" close="]" separators=",">    <mrow>      <mtext>b</mtext>      <mo>+</mo>      <mtext>a</mtext>      <mi>&pi;</mi>    </mrow>  </mfenced></mrow></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Work done by gravity = &ndash; (Change in P.E.)

&rArr; u<sub>i</sub> = u<sub>linear</sub> + u<sub>curved</sub>

<math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>Mass per unit length</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>M</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>a</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>&pi;</mi> <mtext>b</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>W</mtext> <mtext>gravity</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <msub> <mtext>u</mtext> <mtext>i</mtext> </msub> <mo>-</mo> <msub> <mtext>u</mtext> <mtext>f</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>Mgb</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> <mtext>a</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mi>&pi;</mi> <mtext>b</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mfenced close="]" open="[" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>b</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mtext>a</mtext> <mi>&pi;</mi> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>
<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/New_Diagram_2.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" />

</details>

### Q70. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>The system is released form rest with both the springs in unstretched position. Mass of each block is 1 kg and force constant of each spring is 10 N/m. Assume pulleys and strings are massless and all contacts are smooth.Then which of the following is incorrect. (g = 10 m/s<sup>2</sup>)

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0032369_Question.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 203px; height: 203px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Extension of horizontal spring in equilibrium is 2/5 m
- **(B)** Extension of vertical spring in equilibrium is 1/5 m
- **(C)** Maximum speed of the block A is <math> <mrow> <msqrt> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>8</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </msqrt> <mo>m/s</mo> </mrow> </math>
- **(D)** Maximum speed of the block A is <math> <mrow> <msqrt> <mn>1</mn> <mn>4</mn> </msqrt> <mo>m/s</mo> </mrow> </math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Maximum speed of the block A is <math> <mrow> <msqrt> <mn>1</mn> <mn>4</mn> </msqrt> <mo>m/s</mo> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

The system is released...


          <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0032369_Ans_1.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 228px; height: 204px;" />
<strong>    </strong><u><strong><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0032369_Ans_2.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 152px; height: 59px;" /></strong></u><strong>  </strong>             <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0032369_Ans_3.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 152px; height: 163px;" />

 <math> <mrow> <mtext>T</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>kx</mtext> <mo>&rArr;</mo> <mn>5</mn> <mtext>kx</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>mg</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mtext>x</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>mg</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> <mtext>k</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>&times;</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> <mo>&times;</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

(ii) <math> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>g</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>x</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>v</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>v</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mtext>x</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>0.2</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>0.4</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>+</mo> <mn>4</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac>    <mrow>      <mn>1</mn>    </mrow>    <mrow>      <mn>2</mn>    </mrow>  </mfrac> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>0.4</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mtext>1.6</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>&rArr;</mo> <mtext>v</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <msqrt> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>v</mtext> <mtext>A</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>v</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mroot> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mroot> <mo>=</mo> <msqrt> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>8</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>

So only wrong option is (iv)

### Q71. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A mass <math><mi>m</mi> </math> on the surface of the Earth is shifted to a target equal to the radius of the Earth. If <math> <mi>R</mi> </math> is the radius and <math> <mi>M</mi> </math> is the mass of the Earth, then work done in this process is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>R</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>2</mn><mi> </mi><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>R</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Work done <math><mo>=</mo><mo>∆</mo><mi>P</mi><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>G</mi><mi>M</mi><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>G</mi><mi>M</mi><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>G</mi><mi>M</mi><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

---

### Q72. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The potential energy of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> particle free to move along the X-axis is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>4</mn></msup><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>. The total mechanical energy of the particle is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>. Maximum speed of the particle is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** 2

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math> (given)

<math><mo>For maxima or minima of PE =</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>(</mo><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>)</mo></math>

<math><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>x</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> or <math><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

at <math><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi>v</mi><mi>e</mi><mtext>&#8201;&#8201;</mtext><mi>i</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>e</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>,</mo><mtext>&#8201;&#8201;</mtext><mi>a</mi><mi>t</mi><mtext>&#8201;&#8201;</mtext><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo></math>

P.E. is minimum

<math><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow></msub><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mo>&#8756;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>9</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8757;</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math> (given) <math><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math><math><mi>&#160;</mi></math>

</details>

### Q73. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

An object of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> starts to move from rest under the action of a variable force <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math> where <math><mi>x</mi></math> is the distance (in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>) covered by the object. If initially, the position of the object is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>, then find its speed (in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>) when it crosses <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **6**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Increment in kinetic energy = work done</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>u</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mo>&#8747;</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></msubsup><mi>F</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mo>&#8747;</mo><mn>2</mn><mn>10</mn></msubsup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msubsup><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfenced><mn>2</mn><mn>10</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mn>100</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>96</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p> </p>

</details>
